tbmm-2016-11-29sit09spe0598par0300	Thank you, Mr. President. I have made a pledge on behalf of my group regarding Article 50 of the bill in question. I salute the General Assembly. With this bill, you take apprentice training as part of mandatory education, and first, you take a major blow to education, equality and public education, and at the same time, you legitimize child labor. You're taking children out of the basic institution of education and condemning them to work. This is the law of child labor exploitation. While the situation is being assessed in Turkey, it is not overlooked that there is an effort to conceal the promise of the children's labor painting. Since child employment is at 83 per cent unregistered, a significant percentage of the deaths do not reflect on official data. Due to the high level of unregistered employment in child labor, many workplace accidents that cause death and injury do not appear on official record. For this reason, the number of children who died in business accidents and were injured is far higher than the number reflected in official data. According to social security records, 47 children have been claimed to have died in business accidents since 2003. Only one of them is registered under 15 years of age. Again, according to the same source, 17 children lost their lives in work accidents in 2010, where the most deaths occurred. At the same time, 86 children were injured beyond function due to business accidents. Contrary to official information, the investigations and evaluations conducted by the Istanbul Employment Health and Business Security Assembly reveal the reality of the situation. In light of the information obtained in the studies, 1.235 workers who lost their lives in 2013 are 59. 25 of these children work in agriculture, eight of metal, eight of trade, six of construction, four of textiles, two of food, one of chemistry, one of mine, one of cement, one of communication and one of general jobs. So 25 children died in the agriculture sector, 23 children in the industrial sector and 10 in the child service trade sector. In the first four months of 2014, 17 of the 396 workers who lost their lives were child laborers. Five of them are 14 years old and under, and 12 are between 15 and 17 years old. Of the 17 children who lost their lives in the first four months, seven of them work in agriculture, three of them in construction, two in housing, two in general, one in metal, one in trade and one in transport. After the mine disaster in Soma, child workers who worked in the mines were back on the agenda. According to the TEPAV (TİK) data, there are 2,076 children working in coal and lignite mines alone. These kids are 15 and 19. The 85.5% of the children who work in the mine work off the record, and the monthly income they receive is only $226. The problem of child labor and child labor is a multi- factor problem and has become a source of insecurity in Turkey. One of the main reasons why children are involved in working life is poverty and deprivation. Especially because families who migrated from village to town need the money that children bring home because of the shortage of income is one of the most important factors that drives children into working life. It's a structural problem, but the question is, why do employers prefer child labor to run? In fact, the answer is a very simple question because child labor is always cheap labor. They are often preferred by employers because they are working off the books and do not know how to search for their rights, and this is closely related to the complex structure of capitalist modernity, which aims to maximize profit and exploitation. He was brought to the hospital and sentenced to $30,040 in the trial against his employer, and the division of this sentence in 24 taxis is a summary of child labor in Turkey.
